> AGENT

Found the root causes:
1. `SANDCASTLE_HOST="100.106.185.92"` — Tailscale IP, not `hq.sandcastle.rocks`
2. `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="selfsigned"` — self-signed cert, no Let's Encrypt
3. Router rule matches wrong host

```
  SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="${SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE:-selfsigned}"
  SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_USER="${SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_USER:-admin}"

  DOCKYARD_ROOT="${DOCKYARD_ROOT:-$SANDCASTLE_HOME}"
  DOCKYARD_DOCKER_PREFIX="${DOCKYARD_DOCKER_PREFIX:-sc_}"

  # Single RFC 1918 /16 from which all Sandcastle Docker networks are carved.
  # Must be a private range (10.x.x.x, 172.16-31.x.x, or 192.168.x.x).
  SANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET="${SANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET:-10.89.0.0/16}"
  _priv_base="${SANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET%%/*}"        # e.g. "10.89.0.0"
  _priv_prefix="${_priv_base%.*.*}"                  # e.g. "10.89"
  DOCKYARD_BRIDGE_CIDR="${DOCKYARD_BRIDGE_CIDR:-${_priv_prefix}.0.1/24}"
  DOCKYARD_FIXED_CIDR="${DOCKYARD_FIXED_CIDR:-${_priv_prefix}.0.0/24}"
  DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE="${DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE:-${SANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET}}"
  DOCKYARD_POOL_SIZE="${DOCKYARD_POOL_SIZE:-24}"

  DOCKER_SOCK="${DOCKYARD_ROOT}/docker.sock"
  DOCKER="${DOCKYARD_ROOT}/docker-runtime/bin/docker"

  ARCH=$(dpkg --print-architecture 2>/dev/null || echo "amd64")

  # Resolve password from file if needed
  if [ -z "${SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_PASSWORD:-}" ] && [ -n "${SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_PASSWORD_FILE:-}" ]; then
    [ ! -f "$SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_PASSWORD_FILE" ] && die "Password file not found: $SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_PASSWORD_FILE"
    SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_PASSWORD="$(cat "$SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_PASSWORD_FILE")"
  fi
}

# ═══ setup_ssh_keys ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Copy deploying user's SSH keys to sandcastle user

setup_ssh_keys() {
  local deploying_user="${SUDO_USER:-}"

  if [ -z "$deploying_user" ]; then
    warn "No SUDO_USER found — skipping SSH key setup"
    return
  fi

  local source_keys="/home/${deploying_user}/.ssh/authorized_keys"
  local target_dir="${SANDCASTLE_HOME}/.ssh"
  local target_keys="${target_dir}/authorized_keys"

  if [ ! -f "$source_keys" ]; then
    warn "No SSH keys found for user '${deploying_user}' — skipping SSH key setup"
    return
  fi

  info "Setting up SSH keys for '${SANDCASTLE_USER}'..."

  # Create .ssh directory if it doesn't exist
  mkdir -p "$target_dir"
  chmod 700 "$target_dir"
  chown "${SANDCASTLE_USER}:${SANDCASTLE_GROUP}" "$target_dir"

  # Copy authorized_keys
  cp "$source_keys" "$target_keys"
  chmod 600 "$target_keys"
  chown "${SANDCASTLE_USER}:${SANDCASTLE_GROUP}" "$target_keys"

  ok "SSH keys configured for '${SANDCASTLE_USER}'"
}

# ═══ setup_passwordless_sudo ══════════════════════════════════════════════
# Add sandcastle user to sudoers with NOPASSWD:ALL

setup_passwordless_sudo() {
  local sudoers_file="/etc/sudoers.d/sandcastle"

  info "Configuring passwordless sudo for '${SANDCASTLE_USER}'..."

  echo "${SANDCASTLE_USER} ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD:ALL" > "$sudoers_file"
  chmod 440 "$sudoers_file"

  # Validate sudoers syntax
  if ! visudo -cf "$sudoers_file" &>/dev/null; then
    rm -f "$sudoers_file"
    warn "Failed to validate sudoers file — skipping passwordless sudo setup"
    return
  fi

--
  local tls_mode="${SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE:-selfsigned}"

  # Auto-detect host IP
  local host="${SANDCASTLE_HOST:-}"
  if [ -z "$host" ]; then
    host=$(hostname -I 2>/dev/null | tr ' ' '\n' | grep -E '^[0-9]+\.' | head -1 || true)
    host="${host:-$(curl -4fsSL --max-time 5 https://ifconfig.me 2>/dev/null || true)}"
    host="${host:-0.0.0.0}"
  fi

  local admin_email="${SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_EMAIL:-admin@example.com}"
  local admin_user="${SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_USER:-admin}"

  local dy_root="${DOCKYARD_ROOT:-$home}"
  local dy_prefix="${DOCKYARD_DOCKER_PREFIX:-sc_}"
  local priv_net="${SANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET:-$(pick_private_net)}"
  local _pb="${priv_net%%/*}"; local _pp="${_pb%.*.*}"
  local dy_bridge="${DOCKYARD_BRIDGE_CIDR:-${_pp}.0.1/24}"
  local dy_fixed="${DOCKYARD_FIXED_CIDR:-${_pp}.0.0/24}"
  local dy_pool="${DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE:-${priv_net}}"
  local dy_pool_size="${DOCKYARD_POOL_SIZE:-24}"

  cat > "$out" <<EOF
# Sandcastle configuration
# Edit values below, then run: sudo installer.sh install

# ─── Paths & images ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
SANDCASTLE_HOME=${home}
APP_IMAGE=${app_image}
SANDBOX_IMAGE=${sandbox_image}

# ─── System user (home=SANDCASTLE_HOME, shell=/bin/bash) ────────────────────
SANDCASTLE_USER=${user}
SANDCASTLE_GROUP=${group}
SANDCASTLE_UID=${uid}
SANDCASTLE_GID=${gid}

# ─── Network & TLS ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
```

> AGENT

The problem is clear — **all the env files on the server still have the old values**. The `/sandcastle/etc/env` (operator config) and `/sandcastle/.env` both show `SANDCASTLE_HOST=100.106.185.92` and `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE=selfsigned`. Whatever file you edited didn't make it to the server, or the installer was re-run without those changes.

Let me fix it directly — update the configs and restart Traefik:

> AGENT

No backup of a valid acme.json. The good news: **the config is now correct** — Traefik is properly set up with ACME/Let's Encrypt and it's trying. The only blocker is Let's Encrypt's rate limit (5 certs issued this week for `hq.sandcastle.rocks`).

**What happened:** Previous installs/tests on this domain already burned through 5 certificate requests this week, triggering the weekly rate limit.

**Current state:**
- Traefik config: ✅ correct (ACME with `thieso@gmail.com`)  
- `dynamic/rails.yml`: ✅ routes `hq.sandcastle.rocks` with `certResolver: letsencrypt`
- `acme.json`: ✅ empty, 600 perms, ready to receive a cert
- Let's Encrypt: ❌ rate-limited until **2026-02-22 02:18:42 UTC** (~12 hours from now)

Traefik will automatically retry and obtain the cert once the rate limit clears — no manual action needed. The site will start working with a valid SSL cert automatically tomorrow morning.

Until then it'll keep showing the Traefik default self-signed cert. If you need it working NOW, your only option would be to restore an `acme.json` from a previous successful issuance for this domain (if one exists elsewhere).

> AGENT

Done. Here's what was wrong and what was fixed:

**Root cause of today's SSL failure:** Each reinstall did `rm -rf data/traefik`, wiping `acme.json`. The 5 reinstalls this week each re-requested a cert from Let's Encrypt, exhausting the weekly rate limit.

**Fix:** `cmd_destroy` now surgically removes only the regenerable files (`traefik.yml`, `dynamic/`, `certs/`) while leaving `acme.json` intact — same pattern as how `data/postgres` and `data/users` are already preserved. The `rmdir` (not `rm -rf`) ensures the now-non-empty `data/traefik/` dir stays put.

**For the current server:** The rate limit clears at **2026-02-22 02:18:42 UTC** (~12h from now). Traefik will automatically obtain the cert then — no action needed.

> AGENT

Two findings here:
1. The only DB user is `admin` — the `sc-ts-thies` container was a **leftover from a previous install**
2. **The Rails ContainerSyncJob is re-writing the Traefik config back to `100.106.185.92`** — undoing our fix!

Let me check that Traefik startup code:

> AGENT

Good — `hq.sandcastle.rocks` is now being written correctly. Here's the full picture:

**Tailscale diagnosis:**
- `sc-ts-thies` was an **orphan container from a previous install** — the `reset` + reinstall today didn't clean it up since it wasn't tracked by docker compose
- The only user in the current DB is `admin` — no "thies" user exists
- That orphan container likely lost internet when the new install reconfigured the network stack, then eventually died

**To re-enable Tailscale:** you need to create a user named `thies` (or whatever you want) in the web UI at `https://hq.sandcastle.rocks` and enable Tailscale from their settings — once the LE cert arrives (~midnight tonight).

```
commit 64b9e9c7ea05c557186a78ec91d953e75700b8b3
Author: Thies C. Arntzen <thieso@gmail.com>
Date:   Sat Feb 21 15:30:56 2026 +0100

    fix(tailscale): randomize subnet when DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE is unset
    
    Without DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE (e.g. deploy:local), the fallback always
    picked from 10.89.x.0/24. Now both octets are randomized, giving a
    truly random 10.x.y.0/24 subnet for the Tailscale sidecar network.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Entire-Checkpoint: 62502b73fabe
```

```
commit 64b9e9c7ea05c557186a78ec91d953e75700b8b3
Author: Thies C. Arntzen <thieso@gmail.com>
Date:   Sat Feb 21 15:30:56 2026 +0100

    fix(tailscale): randomize subnet when DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE is unset
    
    Without DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE (e.g. deploy:local), the fallback always
    picked from 10.89.x.0/24. Now both octets are randomized, giving a
    truly random 10.x.y.0/24 subnet for the Tailscale sidecar network.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Entire-Checkpoint: 62502b73fabe

diff --git a/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb b/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb
index f4b0221..d7af014 100644
--- a/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb
+++ b/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb
@@ -314,8 +314,12 @@ class TailscaleManager
       # Network doesn't exist yet — fall through to generate a random /24
     end
 
-    base = ENV.fetch("DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE", "10.89.0.0/16")
-    parts = base.split("/").first.split(".").map(&:to_i)
+    base = ENV["DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE"]
+    if base
+      parts = base.split("/").first.split(".").map(&:to_i)
+    else
+      parts = [ 10, rand(1..254), 0, 0 ]
+    end
     parts[2] = rand(1..254)
     "#{parts[0]}.#{parts[1]}.#{parts[2]}.0/24"
   end
---
commit 4fdf83f33ef9499ad78d7b1d14775b6425e4f1e1
Author: Thies C. Arntzen <thieso@gmail.com>
Date:   Sat Feb 21 15:18:43 2026 +0100

    feat(installer): randomize private network in gen-env
    
    Add pick_private_net() which selects a random 10.X.0.0/16 that doesn't
    conflict with existing routes on the host, instead of always defaulting
    to 10.89.0.0/16. Each gen-env run now produces a unique network range.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Entire-Checkpoint: 62502b73fabe

 installer.sh              | 22 +++++++++++++++++++++-
 installer/installer.sh.in | 22 +++++++++++++++++++++-
 2 files changed, 42 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)
commit 03ea177e626964fa57e22cb0f4fee3608b79fdd2
Author: Thies C. Arntzen <thieso@gmail.com>
Date:   Sat Feb 21 14:17:04 2026 +0100

    fix(tailscale): random subnet per install instead of deterministic user-id-based
    
    subnet_for() now:
    1. If the Tailscale bridge network already exists, reads its actual
       subnet from the Docker IPAM config (stable across container restarts)
    2. If not, picks a random /24 from within DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE
    
    Previously user_id 1 always → 10.89.2.0/24 on every install, so two
    Sandcastle instances would advertise the same subnet to the same tailnet
    and both sandboxes would get IP 10.89.2.3 — causing routing conflicts
    and confusing CLI output.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Entire-Checkpoint: 62502b73fabe

 app/services/tailscale_manager.rb | 15 ++++++++++-----
 1 file changed, 10 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)
```

```
2026-02-20T17:56:34.044242693Z

=== subnet_for() in running image ===
  def subnet_for(user)
    base = ENV.fetch("DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE", "10.89.0.0/16")
    # Derive per-user /24 from the /16 pool allocated by dockyard
    # e.g. base 10.89.0.0/16, user_id 1 → 10.89.1.0/24, user_id 2 → 10.89.2.0/24
    # This ensures Tailscale networks are within DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE so dockyard's NAT rules apply
    parts = base.split("/").first.split(".").map(&:to_i)
    offset = 1 + (user.id % 255)  # 1-255 to avoid .0 subnet collision
    parts[2] = offset
    "#{parts[0]}.#{parts[1]}.#{parts[2]}.0/24"
  end

  def create_network(name, subnet)
    Docker::Network.get(name)
  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
    Docker::Network.create(
      name,
```

> AGENT

The running image is from Feb 20 — **pre-fix**. And more importantly, `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` is **not passed to the Rails container** in docker-compose.yml, so if the Dockyard pool isn't `10.89.0.0/16`, `subnet_for()` would generate a subnet outside the MASQUERADE range. That's how `10.159.29.0/24` happened.

Two things to fix:
1. Pass `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` to Rails in docker-compose.yml
2. The installer template needs to do the same

> TOOL

> AGENT

Done. Here's the full picture of what was wrong:

**Root cause:** The old `sc-ts-thies` Tailscale container was created when `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` wasn't passed to the Rails container. The `subnet_for()` code either used a random fallback or a pre-pool formula, landing on `10.159.29.0/24` — a subnet with no MASQUERADE rule → no internet.

**Fixes applied:**
1. `docker-compose.yml` on the server now passes `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE=10.89.0.0/16` to web+worker
2. Template updated so future installs always have it
3. Committed and pushed (`983433b`)

**Next steps for you:** The `sc-ts-thies` container is completely gone. To get Tailscale working again, enable it for your user via the web UI — new containers will use `10.89.x.0/24` which IS covered by the Dockyard MASQUERADE rule and will have full internet.

> DEVELOPER

do i need new images or is reinstalling enogh?

> AGENT

Neither — the server is **already fixed and running correctly**. Here's why:

**What needed to happen:**
1. `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` passed to the Rails container → ✅ done (patched docker-compose.yml + restarted)
2. The running image's `subnet_for()` uses `ENV.fetch("DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE", "10.89.0.0/16")` — now that the env var IS set, it works correctly

**What the running image will generate** for the admin user (id=1):
- `10.89.2.0/24` → inside the Dockyard MASQUERADE range → internet ✅

**What a new image would add** (the `03ea177` + `64b9e9c` commits): random subnets instead of deterministic user-id-based ones. Not needed for Tailscale to work — just a nice-to-have for multi-install isolation. A new image would be needed when you do the next release anyway.

**Just re-enable Tailscale via the web UI** — the new sidecar container will come up with a `10.89.x.0/24` subnet and have full internet.

     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→Sandcastle is a self-hosted shared Docker sandbox platform. Users get isolated Sysbox containers with SSH access and a full Docker daemon inside. The stack is Rails 8.1 (Ruby 4.0) for the web/API backend and a Go CLI for user interaction.
     8→
     9→> **Important:** The container runtime is **Docker + Sysbox** (`sysbox-runc`). Incus/LXD is **not** used and should never be introduced. All container operations go through the `docker-api` gem via the Docker socket.
    10→
    11→## Commands
    12→
    13→### Rails App
    14→
    15→```bash
    16→bin/dev                              # Start dev server (web + Tailwind watcher via foreman)
    17→bin/rails test                       # Run all tests (Minitest)
    18→bin/rails test test/models/user_test.rb          # Run a single test file
    19→bin/rails test test/models/user_test.rb:10       # Run a single test by line
    20→bin/rails db:migrate                 # Run pending migrations
    21→bin/rails db:prepare                 # Migrate + seed
    22→bin/rubocop                          # Lint (rubocop-rails-omakase style)
    23→bin/brakeman                         # Security scan
    24→```
    25→
    26→### Go CLI (vendor/sandcastle-cli/)
    27→
    28→```bash
    29→cd vendor/sandcastle-cli && make build    # Build binary → ./sandcastle
    30→cd vendor/sandcastle-cli && go build ./...  # Quick compile check
    31→```
    32→
    33→Go module path is `github.com/sandcastle/cli`. When adding a new feature: add types to `api/types.go`, client methods to `api/client.go`, then the Cobra command in `cmd/`.
    34→
    35→### Patching Live
    36→
    37→To apply a single file change directly to the running production instance without a full redeploy:
    38→
    39→```bash
    40→scp path/to/file.rb sandcastle@sandman:/tmp/file.rb
    41→ssh sandcastle@sandman 'docker cp /tmp/file.rb sandcastle-web:/rails/path/to/file.rb && docker restart sandcastle-web'
    42→```
    43→
    44→### Full CI
    45→
    46→```bash
    47→bin/ci    # Runs rubocop → brakeman → bundler-audit → importmap audit → tests → system tests
    48→```
    49→
    50→### Releasing

```
   143→### Tailscale System
   144→
   145→Per-user Tailscale sidecars connect sandbox containers to a user's own tailnet. Each user gets their own sidecar joining **their** tailnet (not a shared one).
   146→
   147→**Two authentication flows in `TailscaleManager`:**
   148→- **Auth key flow** (`enable`): One-shot with `TS_AUTHKEY` env var, uses default `containerboot` entrypoint
   149→- **Interactive login flow** (`start_login` → `check_login`): Overrides entrypoint to `tailscaled` directly, runs `tailscale up --reset --timeout=10s` to get a browser login URL, polls `tailscale status --json` for `BackendState == "Running"`
   150→
   151→**User `tailscale_state` field:** `disabled` → `pending` (during interactive login) → `enabled`
   152→
   153→**Sidecar architecture:**
   154→- Bridge network: `sc-ts-net-{username}` with subnet `172.{100+user_id%100}.0.0/16`
   155→- Container: `sc-ts-{username}` running `tailscale/tailscale:latest`
   156→- State persisted at `/data/users/{name}/tailscale`
   157→- Advertises subnet routes so sandboxes on the bridge are reachable from the tailnet
   158→- `tailscale_auto_connect` user setting auto-joins new sandboxes to the bridge network
   159→
   160→**Important:** `containerboot` (default Tailscale image entrypoint) conflicts with manual `tailscale up` when no auth key is provided. The interactive flow must override the entrypoint to run `tailscaled` directly.
   161→
   162→### Background Jobs
   163→
   164→Solid Queue with recurring schedule in `config/recurring.yml`:
   165→- `ContainerSyncJob` — every 5 min, reconciles DB sandbox status with actual Docker container state and Tailscale sidecar health
   166→
   167→### Database
   168→
   169→PostgreSQL with 4 separate databases in production (`config/database.yml`):
   170→- `sandcastle_production` — primary (users, sandboxes, api_tokens, sessions)
   171→- `sandcastle_production_cache` — Solid Cache
   172→- `sandcastle_production_queue` — Solid Queue
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/CLAUDE.md",
  "old_string": "**Sidecar architecture:**\n- Bridge network: `sc-ts-net-{username}` with subnet `172.{100+user_id%100}.0.0/16`\n- Container: `sc-ts-{username}` running `tailscale/tailscale:latest`\n- State persisted at `/data/users/{name}/tailscale`\n- Advertises subnet routes so sandboxes on the bridge are reachable from the tailnet\n- `tailscale_auto_connect` user setting auto-joins new sandboxes to the bridge network\n\n**Important:** `containerboot` (default Tailscale image entrypoint) conflicts with manual `tailscale up` when no auth key is provided. The interactive flow must override the entrypoint to run `tailscaled` directly.",
  "new_string": "**Sidecar architecture:**\n- Bridge network: `sc-ts-net-{username}` with a random `/24` subnet drawn from `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` (e.g. `10.89.42.0/24`)\n- Container: `sc-ts-{username}` running `tailscale/tailscale:latest`\n- State persisted at `/data/users/{name}/tailscale`\n- Advertises subnet routes so sandboxes on the bridge are reachable from the tailnet\n- `tailscale_auto_connect` user setting auto-joins new sandboxes to the bridge network\n\n**Important:** `containerboot` (default Tailscale image entrypoint) conflicts with manual `tailscale up` when no auth key is provided. The interactive flow must override the entrypoint to run `tailscaled` directly.\n\n### Networking Invariants — Do Not Change Without Asking\n\nThe Dockyard Docker daemon (`sc_docker`) and the Rails app share a single private address space. **All of the following must stay consistent or Tailscale sidecars (and sandbox containers) will lose internet access.**\n\n```\nSANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET  (e.g. 10.89.0.0/16)\n└── DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE  = same /16  (passed to dockerd --default-address-pool)\n    ├── DOCKYARD_BRIDGE_CIDR  = first /24 (e.g. 10.89.0.1/24) — sc_docker0 bridge\n    ├── DOCKYARD_FIXED_CIDR   = same /24 (e.g. 10.89.0.0/24)  — static container IPs\n    ├── sandcastle-web network — allocated from the pool (e.g. 10.89.1.0/24)\n    └── sc-ts-net-{user}      — random /24 from the pool (e.g. 10.89.42.0/24)\n```\n\n**iptables rules** (set by the `sc_docker` systemd unit at startup, NOT by dockerd):\n- `POSTROUTING MASQUERADE` for the entire `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` /16 → gives all containers internet via NAT\n- `FORWARD ACCEPT` for src/dst within the /16 → allows container-to-container and container-to-internet traffic\n\n**Critical constraints:**\n1. `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` **must be passed** to the Rails `web` and `worker` containers as an env var — `subnet_for()` in `TailscaleManager` reads it to pick a subnet within the masqueraded range. If it is missing or mismatched, Tailscale sidecars get a subnet outside the MASQUERADE rule and have no internet.\n2. The Dockyard daemon runs with `--iptables=false` — it never touches iptables itself. All rules live in the systemd unit. **Do not add Docker iptables management** (`--iptables=true`) without updating the unit's ExecStartPre rules accordingly.\n3. `SANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET` / `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` must not overlap with the host's existing routes. `gen-env` picks a non-conflicting /16 automatically — do not hard-code a range without checking.\n4. Changing `SANDCASTLE_PRIVATE_NET` on an existing install requires destroying all containers and networks first — existing containers have IPs baked in."
}
```

```
   510→   <section class="mb-10">
   511→     <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">6. Web terminal</h2>
   512→     <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   513→-      Click the <strong>Terminal</strong> button on any running sandbox to open a browser-based shell.
   514→+      Click <strong>tmux</strong> or <strong>shell</strong> on any running sandbox to open a browser-based terminal.
   515→       No SSH client needed — just a web browser.
   516→     </p>
   517→     <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 text-sm text-blue-800">
   518→-      <strong>How it works:</strong> Sandcastle spins up an ephemeral WeTTY container that auto-connects to your sandbox via SSH.
   519→-      The terminal session is protected by your existing login — no extra passwords required.
   520→+      <strong>How it works:</strong> ttyd runs inside each sandbox container, exposing two terminal endpoints:
   521→+      <strong>tmux</strong> (persistent, re-attachable session shared across browser tabs) and
   522→+      <strong>shell</strong> (a plain login shell).
   523→+      Both are protected by your existing login — no extra passwords required.
   524→     </div>

   557→--- a/app/views/terminal/wait.html.erb

   559→@@ -31,7 +31,7 @@
   560→ 
   561→ <script>
   562→   (function() {
   563→-    var statusUrl = "<%= terminal_status_sandbox_path(@sandbox) %>";
   564→+    var statusUrl = "<%= terminal_status_sandbox_path(@sandbox, type: @type) %>";
   565→     var terminalUrl = "<%= j @terminal_url %>";

   568→diff --git a/images/sandbox/Dockerfile b/images/sandbox/Dockerfile
   569→index 258ee84..48685bc 100644
   570→--- a/images/sandbox/Dockerfile
   571→+++ b/images/sandbox/Dockerfile
   572→@@ -81,6 +81,13 @@ RUN mkdir -p /opt/sandcastle/bin \
   573→     && cp -L /root/.local/bin/claude /opt/sandcastle/bin/claude \
   574→     && rm -rf /root/.local/share/claude /root/.local/bin/claude
   575→ 
   576→+# ttyd — web terminal (two ports: 7681=tmux, 7682=shell)
   577→+RUN ARCH=$(dpkg --print-architecture) \
   578→+    && TTYD_ARCH=$([ "$ARCH" = "arm64" ] && echo "aarch64" || echo "x86_64") \
   579→+    && curl -fsSL "https://github.com/tsl0922/ttyd/releases/latest/download/ttyd.${TTYD_ARCH}" \
   580→+       -o /usr/local/bin/ttyd \
   581→+    && chmod +x /usr/local/bin/ttyd
   582→+
   583→ # Entire CLI — git observability for AI agent sessions (https://entire.io)
   584→ RUN ARCH="$(dpkg --print-architecture)" \
   585→     && curl -fsSL "https://github.com/entireio/cli/releases/latest/download/entire_linux_${ARCH}.tar.gz" \
   586→@@ -116,6 +123,6 @@ LABEL org.opencontainers.image.description="Sandcastle sandbox image — Ubuntu
   587→ LABEL org.opencontainers.image.licenses="MIT"
   588→ 
   589→ WORKDIR /workspace
   590→-EXPOSE 22 5900 6080
   591→+EXPOSE 22 5900 6080 7681 7682
   592→ 
   593→ ENTRYPOINT ["/entrypoint.sh"]
   594→diff --git a/images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh b/images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh
   595→index 537be18..ae03571 100644
   596→--- a/images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh
   597→+++ b/images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh
   598→@@ -103,5 +103,17 @@ if command -v Xvnc &>/dev/null && [ "$VNC_ENABLED" = "1" ]; then
   599→     echo "DISPLAY=:99" >> /etc/environment
   600→ fi
   601→ 
   602→+# Start ttyd web terminals (run as sandbox user)
   603→+# Port 7681: tmux session (persistent, re-attaches; unlimited clients)
   604→+# Port 7682: plain login shell (one client at a time)
   605→+if command -v ttyd &>/dev/null; then
   606→+    touch /var/log/ttyd-tmux.log /var/log/ttyd-shell.log
   607→+    chown "$USERNAME:$USERNAME" /var/log/ttyd-tmux.log /var/log/ttyd-shell.log
   608→+    su -s /bin/bash "$USERNAME" -c \
   609→+        "ttyd -W -m 0 -p 7681 tmux new-session -A -s main &>/var/log/ttyd-tmux.log &"
   610→+    su -s /bin/bash "$USERNAME" -c \
   611→+        "ttyd -W -m 1 -p 7682 bash -l &>/var/log/ttyd-shell.log &"
   612→+fi
   613→+
   614→ # Start SSH daemon in foreground
   615→ exec /usr/sbin/sshd -D -e
```

> AGENT

This is a major architectural change — WeTTY replaced by `ttyd` running inside the sandbox. Let me commit it properly:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01VGoUQJWKDedVZH7YVbQA9p
```json
{
  "command": "git add \\\n  app/controllers/terminal_controller.rb \\\n  app/services/terminal_manager.rb \\\n  app/views/dashboard/_sandbox.html.erb \\\n  app/views/pages/guide.html.erb \\\n  app/views/sandboxes/show.html.erb \\\n  app/views/terminal/wait.html.erb \\\n  images/sandbox/Dockerfile \\\n  images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh \\\n  test/controllers/terminal_controller_test.rb && \\\ngit rm app/jobs/terminal_open_job.rb && \\\ngit commit -m \"feat(terminal): replace WeTTY sidecar with ttyd running inside sandbox\n\nInstead of spinning up an ephemeral WeTTY container that SSH-tunnels into\nthe sandbox, ttyd now runs directly inside each sandbox container at boot:\n\n  Port 7681 — tmux (persistent, re-attachable session, unlimited clients)\n  Port 7682 — shell (plain login shell, one client at a time)\n\nTraefik proxies /terminal/{id}/tmux and /terminal/{id}/shell directly to\nthe sandbox container by name, with forwardAuth for session validation.\n\nBenefits:\n- No WeTTY container lifecycle, SSH keypair generation, or pubkey injection\n- No background job (TerminalOpenJob deleted) — ttyd is already running\n- TerminalManager reduced from 340 → 63 lines\n- Two terminal modes accessible from dashboard and sandbox show page\n- active?() uses docker exec + ss to probe the port from inside the container\n\nSandbox image: installs ttyd binary, exposes ports 7681/7682, entrypoint\nstarts both ttyd processes as the sandbox user.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\" && \\\ngit push",
  "description": "Commit and push the WeTTY-to-ttyd terminal rewrite"
}
```

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. Here's what happened:

**Root cause summary:**
1. `sc-ts-thies` was an orphan container from a **previous install** (when admin username was "thies") on subnet `10.159.29.0/24` — outside the MASQUERADE range
2. When `sandcastle-web` restarted with the `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` fix, `ContainerSyncJob` found the orphan was gone and marked tailscale as `disabled`
3. Currently there's only one user `admin` with `tailscale_state: disabled`
4. No Tailscale container or network exists at all

**The underlying bug is fixed** — `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE=10.89.0.0/16` is now correctly in the web container, so any new Tailscale sidecar will get a subnet in `10.89.x.0/24`, which is covered by the MASQUERADE rule.

**To restore Tailscale:** Go to the Settings page in the web UI and re-enable Tailscale (auth key or interactive login). This will create `sc-ts-admin` on a `10.89.x.0/24` subnet with proper internet access.

If you want to install with username "thies" instead of "admin" so the container names match your SSH username, you can set `SANDCASTLE_ADMIN_USER=thies` in `/sandcastle/etc/env` before the next reinstall.

id: toolu_013bmrDnuuPoKRNQkxx1n5HK
```
# /etc/systemd/system/sc_docker.service
[Unit]
Description=Dockyard Docker (sc_docker)
After=network-online.target nss-lookup.target firewalld.service sc_sysbox.service time-set.target
Before=docker.service
Wants=network-online.target
Requires=sc_sysbox.service
StartLimitBurst=3
StartLimitIntervalSec=60

[Service]
Type=forking
PIDFile=/run/sc_docker/dockerd.pid

# Create directories
ExecStartPre=/bin/mkdir -p /sandcastle/docker-runtime/log /sandcastle/docker-runtime/run /run/sc_docker/containerd /sandcastle/docker/containerd

# Clean stale sockets
ExecStartPre=-/bin/rm -f /run/sc_docker/containerd/containerd.sock /sandcastle/docker.sock

# Create bridge
ExecStartPre=/bin/bash -c 'if ! ip link show sc_docker0 &>/dev/null; then ip link add sc_docker0 type bridge && ip addr add 10.89.0.1/24 dev sc_docker0 && ip link set sc_docker0 up; fi'

# Add iptables rules for container networking (bridge)
ExecStartPre=/bin/bash -c 'iptables -I FORWARD -i sc_docker0 -o sc_docker0 -j ACCEPT && iptables -I FORWARD -i sc_docker0 ! -o sc_docker0 -j ACCEPT && iptables -I FORWARD -o sc_docker0 -m conntrack --ctstate RELATED,ESTABLISHED -j ACCEPT && iptables -t nat -I POSTROUTING -s 10.89.0.0/24 ! -o sc_docker0 -j MASQUERADE'

# Add iptables rules for user-defined networks (from default-address-pool)
ExecStartPre=/bin/bash -c 'iptables -I FORWARD -s 10.89.0.0/16 -j ACCEPT && iptables -I FORWARD -d 10.89.0.0/16 -j ACCEPT && iptables -t nat -I POSTROUTING -s 10.89.0.0/16 -j MASQUERADE'

# Start containerd and wait for socket
ExecStartPre=/bin/bash -c '/sandcastle/docker-runtime/bin/containerd --root /sandcastle/docker/containerd --state /run/sc_docker/containerd --address /run/sc_docker/containerd/containerd.sock &>/sandcastle/docker-runtime/log/containerd.log & echo $! > /sandcastle/docker-runtime/run/containerd.pid; i=0; while [ ! -e /run/sc_docker/containerd/containerd.sock ]; do sleep 1; i=$((i+1)); if [ $i -ge 30 ]; then echo "containerd did not start within 30s" >&2; exit 1; fi; done'

# Start dockerd
ExecStart=/bin/bash -c '/sandcastle/docker-runtime/bin/dockerd --config-file /sandcastle/docker-runtime/etc/daemon.json --containerd /run/sc_docker/containerd/containerd.sock --data-root /sandcastle/docker --exec-root /run/sc_docker --pidfile /run/sc_docker/dockerd.pid --bridge sc_docker0 --fixed-cidr 10.89.0.0/24 --default-address-pool base=10.89.0.0/16,size=24 --host unix:///sandcastle/docker.sock --iptables=false &>/sandcastle/docker-runtime/log/dockerd.log & i=0; while [ ! -e /sandcastle/docker.sock ]; do sleep 1; i=$((i+1)); if [ $i -ge 30 ]; then echo "dockerd did not start within 30s" >&2; exit 1; fi; done'

# Stop containerd
ExecStopPost=-/bin/bash -c 'if [ -f /sandcastle/docker-runtime/run/containerd.pid ]; then kill $(cat /sandcastle/docker-runtime/run/containerd.pid) 2>/dev/null; rm -f /sandcastle/docker-runtime/run/containerd.pid; fi'

# Clean up sockets
ExecStopPost=-/bin/rm -f /sandcastle/docker.sock /run/sc_docker/containerd/containerd.sock

# Remove iptables rules (bridge)
ExecStopPost=-/bin/bash -c 'iptables -D FORWARD -i sc_docker0 -o sc_docker0 -j ACCEPT 2>/dev/null; iptables -D FORWARD -i sc_docker0 ! -o sc_docker0 -j ACCEPT 2>/dev/null; iptables -D FORWARD -o sc_docker0 -m conntrack --ctstate RELATED,ESTABLISHED -j ACCEPT 2>/dev/null; iptables -t nat -D POSTROUTING -s 10.89.0.0/24 ! -o sc_docker0 -j MASQUERADE 2>/dev/null'

# Remove iptables rules (user-defined networks)
ExecStopPost=-/bin/bash -c 'iptables -D FORWARD -s 10.89.0.0/16 -j ACCEPT 2>/dev/null; iptables -D FORWARD -d 10.89.0.0/16 -j ACCEPT 2>/dev/null; iptables -t nat -D POSTROUTING -s 10.89.0.0/16 -j MASQUERADE 2>/dev/null'

# Remove bridge
ExecStopPost=-/bin/bash -c 'if ip link show sc_docker0 &>/dev/null; then ip link set sc_docker0 down 2>/dev/null; ip link delete sc_docker0 2>/dev/null; fi'

TimeoutStartSec=60
TimeoutStopSec=30
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=5

LimitNPROC=infinity
LimitCORE=infinity
LimitNOFILE=infinity
TasksMax=infinity
Delegate=yes
KillMode=process
OOMScoreAdjust=-500

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target
```

```
    47→
    48→  # Phase 2: progressive login status check
    49→  # Returns: { status: "starting" | "waiting_for_url" | "login_ready" | "authenticated" | "error" }
    50→  def check_login(user:)
    51→    raise Error, "No pending login" unless user.tailscale_pending?
    52→    raise Error, "Sidecar container not found" if user.tailscale_container_id.blank?
    53→
    54→    container = Docker::Container.get(user.tailscale_container_id)
    55→    running = container.json.dig("State", "Running")
    56→    return { status: "starting", message: "Starting sidecar container..." } unless running
    57→
    58→    # Kick off `tailscale up` in the background if not already done
    59→    cache_key = "ts_login_started:#{user.id}"
    60→    unless Rails.cache.read(cache_key)
    61→      subnet = subnet_for(user)
    62→      tag = Rails.cache.read("ts_tag:#{user.id}") || TAILSCALE_TAG
    63→      tag_flag = tag ? " --advertise-tags=#{tag}" : ""
    64→      container.exec([
    65→        "sh", "-c",
    66→        "tailscale up --reset" \
    67→        " --advertise-routes=#{subnet}" \
    68→        "#{tag_flag}" \
    69→        " --accept-routes" \
    70→        " --hostname=#{container.json.dig("Config", "Hostname")}" \
    71→        " --timeout=120s &"
    72→      ])
    73→      Rails.cache.write(cache_key, true, expires_in: 5.minutes)
    74→      return { status: "waiting_for_url", message: "Waiting for login URL..." }

    76→
    77→    # Check tailscale status for auth progress

    79→    if status_out.first.any?
    80→      ts_status = JSON.parse(status_out.first.join)
    81→      case ts_status["BackendState"]
    82→      when "Running"
    83→        Rails.cache.delete(cache_key)
    84→        user.update!(tailscale_state: "enabled", tailscale_auto_connect: true)
    85→        ip_out = container.exec([ "tailscale", "ip", "--4" ])

    95→        if auth_url.present?
    96→          return { status: "login_ready", login_url: auth_url, message: "Click the link to authenticate" }
    97→        end
    98→      end
    99→    end
```

```
   229→  private
   230→
   231→  def create_and_start_sidecar(user:, auth_key:)
   232→    network_name = "sc-ts-net-#{user.name}"
   233→    container_name = "sc-ts-#{user.name}"
   234→    subnet = subnet_for(user)
   235→
   236→    pull_image
   237→    create_network(network_name, subnet)
   238→    remove_existing_container(container_name)
   239→    container = create_sidecar(
   240→      name: container_name,
   241→      user: user,
   242→      network: network_name,
   243→      subnet: subnet,
   244→      auth_key: auth_key
   245→    )
   246→    container.start
   247→
   248→    [ network_name, container ]
   249→  end
   250→
   251→  def remove_existing_container(name)
   252→    container = Docker::Container.get(name)
   253→    container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil
   254→    container.delete(force: true)
   255→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   256→    # No existing container
   257→  end
   258→
   259→  def cleanup_sidecar(user)
   260→    # Try by stored container ID first, then by name as fallback
   261→    container_name = "sc-ts-#{user.name}"
   262→    [user.tailscale_container_id, container_name].compact.uniq.each do |ref|
   263→      begin
   264→        container = Docker::Container.get(ref)
   265→        container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil
   266→        container.delete(force: true)
   267→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   268→        # Already gone
   269→      end
   270→    end
   271→
   272→    # Clean up network by stored name, then by convention
   273→    network_name = "sc-ts-net-#{user.name}"
   274→    [user.tailscale_network, network_name].compact.uniq.each do |ref|
   275→      begin
```

> AGENT

Found it! Stale Tailscale state from a previous install (Feb 11). When `tailscaled` starts, it loads the old state, sets up routes through `tailscale0`, and breaks basic internet. Deleting the state and recreating the container will fix it:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01K5azWuSHPrrXdkMqFk1evM

```json
  "command": "ssh thies@100.106.185.92 'sudo rm -rf /data/users/thies/tailscale/tailscaled.state /data/users/thies/tailscale/derpmap.cached.json /data/users/thies/tailscale/files && sudo ls -la /data/users/thies/tailscale/ 2>&1'",
  "description": "Delete stale Tailscale state for thies user"
}
```

> AGENT

Container has internet and Tailscale is running. The login URL is ready:

**https://login.tailscale.com/a/30dae40010689**

Open that in your browser to authenticate the `sc-ts-thies` sidecar to your tailnet.

**Root cause**: The stale `tailscaled.state` from Feb 11 caused `tailscaled` to immediately try to reconnect using old VPN credentials, which set up routing through `tailscale0` before authentication — dropping all normal internet traffic. Fix: cleared `/data/users/thies/tailscale/tailscaled.state` and recreated the container.

**To prevent this in future reinstalls**, the TailscaleManager should clear the state file when it creates a fresh container. But for now, the container is up and waiting for your login.

> AGENT

Internet is **working now** — Tailscale is just not authenticated yet. The earlier outage was caused by `tailscale up --accept-routes` temporarily modifying routing rules while waiting for auth. When it timed out after 30s, normal routing was restored.

The container has internet. You just need to finish the auth. The login URL is still valid:

```
https://login.tailscale.com/a/30dae40010689
```

Open that to authenticate the sidecar to your tailnet.

However, there's a real bug here: **`tailscale up --accept-routes` breaks internet while waiting for login** because it installs routing rules through `tailscale0` before the VPN is established. This is the `check_login` code path in `TailscaleManager`. Let me fix it:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
id: toolu_01AWisqtYZYUdjeq285FBo4k
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb",
  "offset": 58,
  "limit": 18
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01AWisqtYZYUdjeq285FBo4k
```
    58→    # Kick off `tailscale up` in the background if not already done
    59→    cache_key = "ts_login_started:#{user.id}"
    60→    unless Rails.cache.read(cache_key)
    61→      subnet = subnet_for(user)
    62→      tag = Rails.cache.read("ts_tag:#{user.id}") || TAILSCALE_TAG
    63→      tag_flag = tag ? " --advertise-tags=#{tag}" : ""
    64→      container.exec([
    65→        "sh", "-c",
    66→        "tailscale up --reset" \
    67→        " --advertise-routes=#{subnet}" \

    72→      ])
```

> AGENT

The fix: drop `--accept-routes` from the interactive login `tailscale up` command. Routes can only be accepted after the node is authenticated and running anyway — the flag has no effect until then, but `tailscaled` still installs the routing rules that kill internet. The `--advertise-routes` flag is what matters for the subnet routing.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01QhFpzmbrirr3bY8brFJWfB
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "      container.exec([\n        \"sh\", \"-c\",\n        \"tailscale up --reset\" \\\n        \" --advertise-routes=#{subnet}\" \\\n        \"#{tag_flag}\" \\\n        \" --accept-routes\" \\\n        \" --hostname=#{container.json.dig(\"Config\", \"Hostname\")}\" \\\n        \" --timeout=120s &\"\n      ])",
  "new_string": "      container.exec([\n        \"sh\", \"-c\",\n        \"tailscale up --reset\" \\\n        \" --advertise-routes=#{subnet}\" \\\n        \"#{tag_flag}\" \\\n        \" --hostname=#{container.json.dig(\"Config\", \"Hostname\")}\" \\\n        \" --timeout=120s &\"\n      ])"
}
```

id: toolu_01PXPqjqs4SRDsWEGvroZ9i4
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  def remove_existing_container(name)\n    container = Docker::Container.get(name)\n    container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil\n    container.delete(force: true)\n  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n    # No existing container\n  end",
  "new_string": "  def remove_existing_container(name)\n    container = Docker::Container.get(name)\n    container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil\n    container.delete(force: true)\n  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n    # No existing container\n  end\n\n  # Remove stale tailscaled.state so the daemon starts fresh.\n  # Stale state causes tailscaled to immediately set up VPN routes before\n  # authentication, which silently drops all normal internet traffic.\n  def clear_tailscale_state(user)\n    state_file = \"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/tailscale/tailscaled.state\"\n    File.delete(state_file) if File.exist?(state_file)\n  rescue Errno::ENOENT\n    # already gone\n  end"
}
```

```
+  # Remove stale tailscaled.state so the daemon starts fresh.
+  # Stale state causes tailscaled to immediately set up VPN routes before
+  # authentication, which silently drops all normal internet traffic.
+  def clear_tailscale_state(user)
+    state_file = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/tailscale/tailscaled.state"
+    File.delete(state_file) if File.exist?(state_file)
+  rescue Errno::ENOENT
+    # already gone
+  end
+
   def cleanup_sidecar(user)
     # Try by stored container ID first, then by name as fallback
     container_name = "sc-ts-#{user.name}"
```

> AGENT

**Summary of what happened and what was fixed:**

1. **Root cause of "no internet"**: `tailscale up --accept-routes` installs policy routing rules through `tailscale0` before auth is complete. While waiting for login, all traffic was silently dropped. After the 30s timeout, routing was restored — which is why ping started working again mid-debug.

2. **Second root cause**: Stale `tailscaled.state` from Feb 11 (previous install) caused `tailscaled` to immediately try VPN reconnection and set up broken routes on startup.

**Two fixes committed:**
- Removed `--accept-routes` from the interactive login `tailscale up` call
- `clear_tailscale_state()` now deletes `tailscaled.state` when creating a fresh sidecar

The current `sc-ts-thies` container has internet now. Visit **https://login.tailscale.com/a/30dae40010689** to finish authenticating it.
